### 6.6 Experimental feature triage (PLAN-13..40 vs reality)

**Overview (verbatim):** The repo carries ~28 plan documents and roughly a dozen large feature areas, but only a handful are both ON by default, user-visible, and proven to do anything end-to-end: memory (crystals, canonical ledger, SAGE graph, graph-anchored recall), channels, skills (including quarantine/trust and the now-working interceptors), and the dream engine's infrastructure. Everything economic (Forage bounties, Aubaine group-buy, marketplace revenue/reputation, wallet) is either default-off, structurally dead per the 2026-06-09 economy audit and the 2026-08-09 wired-but-dead audit (EigenTrust write-only, revenue split cannot pay a peer, versioning null on the wire), or monitoring-only, yet the README sells it as a working loop. Circles is the most actively developed surface (71 commits, latest 2026-08-17) and is ON by default with a practice-partner bot, canvas sandbox and study-lens UI, while its own security remediation doc (untracked, marked do-not-commit) still lists a live supply-chain critical. The Control UI exposes a reasonable nav, but routes 8 views (Debug RPC caller, Logs, Sessions, Usage, Instances, Nodes, Projects, Workspace) that have no nav entry at all, and the "Dreams (beta)" tab is an iframe of a 10-tab gateway HTML page that includes Forage, Earnings and Live debug panes. Research-only material (ARC-AGI-3 benchmark with 75 tracked files and its tests in the default vitest include, two root-level study documents, research/ references in CI comments) is still in the shipped tree. The plan docs themselves overclaim in several places (PLAN-25 says harness evolution is on by default; the code defaults it off; the README's 12-mode dream table lists 5 modes that are disabled by default). The V1 story is coherent only if the product is reduced to memory + dreams + channels + skills, with Circles and the wallet as explicit opt-ins and the economy described as experimental.

### 6.7 Code health and repo hygiene

**Overview (verbatim):** The source code itself is in far better shape than the repo around it. Across src/ + desktop/renderer/src there are only 3 TODO markers, zero FIXME/HACK, zero heritage names (openclaw/clawdbot/moltbot) in non-test code, and zero console.log in the renderer; most console.log hits in src/ are two dev scripts that live under src/memory/scripts. The repo shell, however, still carries the skeleton of a removed native-app tree (apps/ no longer exists, yet ~12 package.json scripts, 8 mac packaging scripts, the Swift lint configs, .pre-commit hooks, and the A2UI bundler all reference it), a Dockerfile that cannot build (COPY of nonexistent ui/ and patches/, `pnpm ui:build` undefined), a Podman installer whose launch script is missing, and 16 package.json scripts pointing at files that do not exist. 56 MB of benchmark run artifacts (two SQLite DBs plus 53 generated memory .md files) are tracked because .gitignore covers _.db but not _.sqlite. CI is weaker than it looks: `pnpm test:fast` excludes src/gateway/** (95 test files) and extensions/**, renderer tests are never run, and oxlint ignores desktop/ and extensions/ entirely, so the Control UI (the V1 product) has no linter. Large-file debt is real (manager.ts 6472 lines, dream-engine.ts 4293, 26 non-test files over 1000 lines) but a check:loc script exists and is simply not enforced. For V1, the cleanup is mostly deletion and re-wiring, not rewriting.

### 6.8 Security posture for a public V1

**Overview (verbatim):** The core gateway posture is in good shape for a local-first V1: bind defaults to loopback (src/gateway/server-runtime-config.ts:40), startup refuses a non-loopback bind without auth (:91), the wizard always generates a 24-byte random token (src/wizard/onboarding.gateway-config.ts:204-211), config and desktop/.env are written 0600, node identity keys in keys/ and desktop/keys are gitignored and untracked, WS upgrades get an Origin check plus auth rate limiting, sensitive config fields are redacted on the config RPC, exec approvals default to deny + ask-on-miss, DMs default to pairing, the wallet enforces a $50/day cap, and circles.p2pDial is default OFF with mesh rate limiting and relay signing now landed. The weak spots are at the edges rather than the core: the A2A HTTP surface waives auth for every RFC1918/CGNAT source (not just loopback), the Control UI design bakes the gateway token into the JS bundle and leaks it into an iframe URL, unauthenticated loopback GETs to /management and /m hand back the raw gateway token, a dead demo-config.json with security=full/ask=off/dangerouslyDisableDeviceAuth=true is bundled into Tauri resources, the end-user orchestrator download has checksum-only verification, and SECURITY.md points at a docs path that does not exist while claiming CI secret scanning that only runs as a pre-commit hook. None of these is a remote-unauthenticated compromise on the default loopback install, but several would be embarrassing in a public V1 and two become real exposures the moment a user picks bind=lan. Dependency hygiene is absent (no dependabot, no audit step in CI). An untracked remediation doc that explicitly says "do not commit" sits inside docs/reviews and is one `git add -A` away from publishing a vulnerability list.

### 6.9 Packaging, versioning, release engineering

**Overview (verbatim):** Today the only install path that actually works is "git clone + bash scripts/setup-deps.sh + pnpm install + pnpm bitterbot onboard", and even that is currently degraded: orchestrator/Cargo.toml was bumped to 0.2.3 but no orchestrator-v0.2.3 GitHub release exists, so every fresh install silently ends up without the P2P binary. The repo carries three inherited-but-dead distribution stories on top of that: an npm package (never published; registry returns Not found; RELEASING.md and docs point at a nonexistent bitterbot.ai/install.sh and npm install -g), a Swift/Sparkle macOS app toolchain (10 scripts + package.json entries targeting apps/macos, which does not exist), and a Dockerfile that references ui/, patches/ and a pnpm ui:build script that do not exist. The Tauri shell is half-built (no icons dir, updater pubkey placeholder, main.rs still spawns node scripts/run-node.mjs) and should be explicitly out of V1 scope. Versioning is incoherent: package.json is still the upstream-inherited CalVer 2026.2.15 (never bumped since the initial commit), tauri.conf.json says 0.1.0, the orchestrator is 0.2.3, and the repo has never had an app tag. The one solid piece of release engineering is orchestrator-release.yml (SHA-pinned actions, approval-gated environment, minisign-signed checksums). For V1 the realistic matrix is: a fixed source install with a real one-line installer script, the gateway serving the built Control UI instead of a permanent Vite dev server, a working Docker image, SemVer 1.0.0 with a single version-sync script, and everything else deleted or hidden.

### 6.10 Documentation and help

**Overview (verbatim):** The docs surface is two products stapled together. README.md (471 lines) tells the real Bitterbot story accurately (clone + setup-deps.sh + pnpm + `pnpm bitterbot onboard`, Control UI on :5173, gateway on :19001, 12 dream modes, Opus 4.8 default, Circles), while docs/ is still overwhelmingly the inherited multi-channel-gateway doc tree: docs/index.md opens with an "EXFOLIATE!" joke, describes "AI coding agents like Pi", iMessage and Mattermost, and tells users to `npm install -g bitterbot@latest` and open the Control UI at 127.0.0.1:19001, none of which is true for this product. docs/docs.json (Mintlify) references 270 pages that do not exist (48 in the English nav including the entire Install tab, help/index, help/troubleshooting, help/faq, all nodes/_ pages, reference/credits; plus 216 zh-Hans/ja-JP entries with zero translated files), while 119 real pages are orphaned from nav, including every docs/memory/_ page, docs/network/circles.md and docs/agents/interceptors.md, i.e. exactly the pages that differentiate the product. The hosted docs site does not exist (docs.bitterbot.ai fails DNS) even though 81 source files print docs.bitterbot.ai links in CLI help and the agent system prompt, and the Getting Started page pipes https://bitterbot.ai/install.sh (an HTML marketing page) into bash. Nine internal audit/review documents (wired-but-dead audit, economy audit, security remediation, PLAN-40 scorecards) are tracked inside the public docs tree, and two internal RFC/duplicate files sit at repo root. `node scripts/docs-link-audit.mjs` passes (1233 links OK), but it does not validate docs.json nav entries or image assets, so CI is green while the nav is broken. For V1 the fix is less "write more docs" than "cut the heritage tree down to what is true, put the memory/circles/wallet pages in nav, make install/quick-start match README, and either publish the docs site or stop linking to it".

### 6.11 First-run runtime experience and data layout

**Overview (verbatim):** The data layout is reasonably coherent: everything the TypeScript side owns lands under ~/.bitterbot (config + 5 rotating backups, per-agent SQLite memory, workspace markdown, sessions, logs, tasks/event-journal/checkpoints DBs, identity, orchestrator prebuilt binary), schema migrations are versioned (v1..v62), transactional and idempotent, and `bitterbot reset` / `bitterbot uninstall` exist with dry-run and scoped modes. The rough edges are in what happens the first time the gateway runs. The single most important one for a credible V1: the "biological memory" stack silently requires an OpenAI/Gemini/Voyage embedding key; the `auto` provider never selects the local model unless a local .gguf path is already configured, so an Anthropic-only user boots with memory, dreams, hormones and curiosity all dead behind a single warn line. The second is the ~27-minute cold boot, which the real logs pin to a silent synchronous span between the `config-load` and `load-plugins` boot steps (memory init itself is ~2s); the boot-profiling labels that would name the culprit are dropped by the console formatter. Third, the P2P identity keypair is written to `<cwd>/keys/` (the Rust default) because the gateway never passes `--key-dir`, so it lives in the repo checkout, is missed by reset/uninstall, and changes if the gateway is started from a different directory. Beyond those, first boot is noisy and outward-facing by default (P2P relays, circles mailbox, A2A, dream cycle at +5 min against a hard-coded `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, update `git fetch`), success messages are logged at warn, and several stores (event journal, curator reports, skills-incoming) grow without retention while logs are pruned after 24h.

Note from verification: step 4's attribution to the hormonal accessor was corrected; the structured log pins the stall to the synchronous `load-plugins` step itself (see 3.10).

#### Maintenance / lifecycle commands

- `bitterbot doctor [--repair|--fix|--deep|--json]` (`src/cli/program/register.maintenance.ts:12-46`), includes state-integrity, memory-search probe (sqlite-vec), boot-health.
- `bitterbot reset --scope config|config+creds+sessions|full [--dry-run]` (`src/commands/reset.ts`): full = state dir + config + oauth + workspace dirs. Misses `<cwd>/keys/` and `/tmp` socket.
- `bitterbot uninstall --service|--state|--workspace|--app|--all` (`src/commands/uninstall.ts`): `--app` removes `/Applications/Bitterbot.app` (no released app).
- `bitterbot update` (`src/cli/update-cli/update-command.ts`) + boot-verify beacon (30 min deadline, `src/infra/boot-verify.ts:40`) + detached `boot-watchdog` auto-rollback (`git reset --hard`, refuses dirty tree, kill switch `update.autoRollback.enabled=false`) + hidden `ui-restart`. No DB backup before update; migrations forward-only with N-1 policy.
- Hidden internal commands: `boot-watchdog`, `ui-restart`.

### 6.12 Channels, extensions, and model-provider readiness

**Overview (verbatim):** The channel substrate is real and in decent shape: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, and Signal ship as bundled extensions that are enabled by default, register at boot, and get a Control UI card with guided credential setup (validate-before-save, hot restart, QR pairing for WhatsApp/Signal, host-capability greying for Signal when signal-cli is missing). Models & Keys gives a genuine bring-your-own-key path (add/test/rotate/delete, provenance of which source wins) and the wizard offers ~24 provider groups. The problems are mostly honesty and scope, not engineering: README and docs/channels claim Google Chat, Microsoft Teams, IRC, and Matrix channels that have no code anywhere in the repo; iMessage sits in the core channel registry and CLI/wizard choices with a "work in progress" blurb but no plugin ever registers it; ~46 files of LINE channel code and the @line/bot-sdk dependency are dead; Twitch is bundled and enabled by default while its docs say it is not bundled, and it is the only visible channel card with no setup button. Provider docs contradict the README (Venice is "highlighted" while Anthropic is the documented default), link to a missing LiteLLM page, and mention Mattermost. Local models via Ollama work at the engine level but are awkward: there is no wizard or UI path, and the user must invent a fake OLLAMA_API_KEY. Three helper plugins (device-pair, phone-control, talk-voice) are enabled by default and register /pair, /phone, /voice slash commands for mobile node apps and ElevenLabs voices that the V1 product does not ship. Channel docs (most last touched 2026-03-28) never mention the Control UI setup flow, so the best path to configure a channel is undocumented.
